> ​@Merkadiusboth can be true at the same time. And were true at the same time. You would probably like to read a more than 100 year old thesis by Sure Bolin, called "Mohammed, Charlemagne and Ruric".  
>   
> The old Norse were both important for many developments at the time, but also for Vikings (a job description) and destruction. Where I very much disagree with your tutor and think he needs his arse slapped by the flat side of a sword (I also practice HEMA), is that he commits one of the worst sins a historian can; he judges the past by the standard of today. If you compare with contemporary culture; everyone took slaves, or bought slaves (who does he think the Vikings sold to?) Everyone looted and pillaged; the Vikings were just better at it, partly due to the ships that made them able to strike everywhere. And they had no respect for institutions of Christianity, cause they believed in different gods. Which also brings us to another important thing; everything we read today was written by the monks who hated the Norse for their different beliefs and attacks on monasteries and churches. If you look at what the Franks did to their non-christian neighbours they are at least as bad, if not worse. They carried out a genocide and forced conversion on the Saxons, the remainder of which sought refuge with the Danes. Which might be part of what started the first "Viking" raids. Counterattacking the threat from Charlemagne. Meanwhile the same Charlemagne fuelled The Frankish Renaissance in arts, culture and learning. It is all quiet complex, and should be studied on its own merit.  
>   
> So, be objective, use source criticism, look at the whole picture, and do not judge them by our standards. What emerges is both wonder and horror, for humanity contains both. And both is utterly interesting.

### **@avihenda** | 👍 2 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgwrJaxsJ0QlE4gPLGB4AaABAg)

I found his comment about viking school funny. I’m Swedish, so from one of the old viking nations. 
I was at university here in Sweden in the early 2000 and living in the international corridor (student accommodation where everyone has their own room in a corridor, but share a kitchen and Livingroom) because they wanted a few swedes in the mix and I got reduced rent for that privilege. 

There was a good mix of nationalities, french, german, chinese, english etc. 

They where all great at reading books and memorising stuff, but as soon as something went wrong practically in their environment, they where helpless. 

I surprised so many of them when something broke and instead of calling a janitor and hoping they would show up with in a few days, I just went in to my room and came out with tools, and fixed the thing so I could  continue to do what I was doing. 
It was never complicated stuff either, a door handle fell of, a toilet lock jammed, a screw needed tightened or blinds broken. 

When I moved out of the house, my dad gifted me a basic tool set, that I still have. He would never dream of letting any of us kids move out with out a basic tool kit for easy repairs and he would question our mental facilities if we could not do basic problem solving with everyday household stuff. 
I’m a woman and he also made sure we all know how to change tires, navigate in a city, forest and sea and so on. Swedish school tries their best to make sure you have practical skills like woodworking, sewing, everyday repairs, dump entire schools of young kids into the local forest and give them assignments to complete (friluftsdagar). 

I never reflected on how self sufficient we nordics still are, until I spent those years in the international corridor at university and saw top students be helpless over small practical problems in their living area. 
It was eye opening. 

We don’t have viking school, but I think growing up in the nordics, especially in the smaller towns, is the closest you will come. 

I married a dutch guy and he never studied at a higher level. We settled in Sweden and in the beginning he kept asking me if he was really allowed to do stuff and had never held a hammer before. We bought a wooden house and I had him open some walls up, showed him how to identify a bearing wall, gave him a basic this is a house and this is how you repair it book in swedish and showed him how to problem solve a house repair. It’s now 15 years later and he’s built the kids bedroom and our hallway in the cottage, replaced the roof and all the floors himself and last week he opened up a wall to see what was going on, found the issue and rebuilt it again. Very proud of him. 
He now gets annoyed with his mum because she will pay a painter to repaint windows in holland. His integration is now complete. 😂😂😂

### **@Jdanielsson76** | 👍 7 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=Ugx33aQgWJonUX9jYkl4AaABAg)

In the swedish language we still separate between our ancestral lines so it can be sewn into a tapestry, what I mean with this is that we seperate our grandparents, for example farfar vs morfar, which simply is fathers father vs mothers father, or for grandmother it would be farmor vs mormor, uncles morbror vs farbror. This allows us to build long family chains where for example I could refer to one of my ancestors as: mormors morfars farfars farfars farsfars morfars far. I would argue that this is due to the svear being more of a trading culture, where relational networks of trust are much more important than in a settled agricultural culture, where land inheretence is key. In swedish the word lag also means both team and law, and laglös would translate into without team/law i.e. outlaw.

I went to viking school lol...I was raised off grid on a mountain top. Very little public school though I learned to read and write, do math, learn how our government works,  learned basic science,  algebra,  ect. Mostly I learned to cut firewood with a bow saw, ax, or chainsaw.  Grow every crop organically you can imagine.  Can or preserve the same food I grew, farm chickens for eggs and use their fertilizer for food crops. Basic home construction, fix any automobile or gas powered engine. A bunch of other stuff too. One major drawback to my upbringing is I didn't learn about people and have always been very shy and completely unable to feel comfortable with strangers and crowds. Public speaking is basically impossible for me. Also I live in a world in the city where what I learned as a kid is mostly all completely worthless.  I was prepared for a world that mostly doesn't exist any more....

From an Anglo-Saxon perspective, the Norsemen had a great degree of influence on our language, laws, and culture.  Most of our days of the week are named after their gods, which were essentially the same gods worshipped by the Angles, Saxons and Jutes.  So many English words we take for granted today have a Norse origin.  Political terms like "Ombudsman" are Norse.  In Scots and Northern dialects of English, a child is a "Bairn" which is a cognate of their word "Barn".  I too have always felt their influence on "western" civilisation is under appreciated.  As a Bairn ( ;) )  I was always pulled towards what survives of our pre christian traditions from a place of both curiosity and respect for my forebears.  Modern Christmas (Jule/Yule) is not really very Christian, but is basically a hold over from the Germanic/Norse religion.  One could go on.

> @theoderich1168There is some conflation of Viking and Germanic going on here. And Hellenistic/Roman too. The weekday naming convention in English, for example is based on the Hellenistic planetary system, which was simply translated into Germanic languages in the process called interpretatio germanica. This is in great contrast to Slavic weekday naming conventions, where basically, the days are not named after planet-gods but simply by numbers + Hebrew word for Saturday and 'no-work day' for Sunday.

### **@l_i_f_e_force** | 👍 46 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgwCXcSKlZ9hC3umlJh4AaABAg)

The viking age essentially started as a political uproar against Charlemagne. The Viking Age (793–1066) didn’t just appear out of nowhere. For centuries before it, Scandinavians were already skilled seafarers and traders. They ran networks stretching across Europe, into Russia, and even connecting with the Silk Road. They exported amber, furs, walrus ivory, slaves – and crucially, iron smelted from bogs (myrmalm). Archaeological sites show large-scale iron production in Scandinavia centuries before 800 CE, enough not only for local tools and weapons but also for export. In return they brought back silver, silk, wine, and glass.

The shift came with Charlemagne. As he expanded his empire in the late 700s, subduing Saxons and Frisians while enforcing Christianization, he tightened control over Northern Europe’s trade arteries. The Rhine and North Sea routes came under Frankish tolls, taxes, and blockages. Norse merchants, who had enjoyed relatively free commerce with the Franks, suddenly lost access to their main markets and sources of wealth.

Historians often point to three big factors: Charlemagne’s expansion threatened both Norse religion and economics, his customs and toll barriers cut into long-standing trade routes, and within Scandinavia ambitious chieftains needed silver and prestige goods to secure power.

So when trade was strangled, raiding became the alternative. The first major raid at Lindisfarne in 793 happened right as Charlemagne consolidated control over the north. In that sense, the Viking Age wasn’t a sudden outbreak of piracy, but a forced shift: skilled traders and iron-producers pushed into raiding because the Frankish empire closed off their traditional trade.

### **@eb4661** | 👍 77 likes

This lecture is by far the best I’ve seen on this topic.

As a Norwegian in my fifties, I have had my fair share of history lessons. I have also read most of the sagas and visited many of the most notable places, and I have rowed and sailed many a small wooden boat made on the old viking traditions - which was in common production only four decades ago. 

There is a foundation prior of the viking age, going back a millennium with the tradition of building large ships:

It takes specialists to organize and build a ship. Hence, it takes a society of hierarchy, trades and taxes to fund and feed the builders. Close to where I live, there is a Shipawick (a place where ships were built and set to sea). We know orders went out for a variety of different sub-categories and items needed for a ship, to local headsmen/farmers in perhaps a 100 square kilometer area. All would have to deliver according to plans, semifinished goods, as planks, ropes, bends, or thousands and thousands of nails. 

Some parts of the pre-viking and viking culture must have survived and lives with us today, especially in Norway. An example is the how Norwegians organized the oil-business that suddenly came up as a potential sixty years ago. Expanded borders on the sea floor, agreed multilaterally, development of local skills, infrastructure and businesses. All was aimed to improve for all, not the few, and never to be exploited foreigners. The waterfalls and electricity prior of this was a success for all too. And, so is the pension funds, currently owning a percent or so of all stocks on the major stock exchanges.

When it comes to language, it is the same. The days of the week are mostly norse gods, the moon and the sun, and then a day to wash. “Stocks” is Norwegian too, a marking system for traders where proof of ownership could be passed without moving the goods from storage. Starbord, is the bord on a viking ship of the right side, used to steer the ship. Etc etc

The timeline is incorrect. It's based too much on English sources and dates. One can when it comes to the culture also incorporate the Vendel period, that is 550 to 750, since it has most of the characteristics of the Viking period already present. Apart from that raids had already been going on in the Baltics for some time before the attack on Lindisfarne. Recently they have found a burial of two ships on an Island outside Estonia, Saaremaa island, dating to 750, with Viking warriors killed in action. And when it comes to the end of the period, a more correct date would be at a battle in the East where an eyewitness chronicle mentions Vikings present. And that was as late as 1250 or around there. So the period of the culture in itself can be dated from 550 to 1250. At most. And Lindisfarne was not the first attack on England, at least one was earlier. I dont remember the date but perhaps 760 or thereabout.

Here's a question for all of us who watch these lectures (and engage with the concepts presented) : if the vikings made their mark so powerfully on the European world ( which they did - this is not disputed ), is that the equivalent of the prestige assigned to the Scandinavian countries and models today?
And here's where the thought originated : I have been pondering on why has the condition of the worker declined significantly since the '70. While there are a lot of factors that contribute and create the answer, I think one of the main reasons is the fall of communism in the '90s. Before anyone jumps on the subject, I am not advocating for communism under any circumstance ( I was born in a communist, eastern European country, in a family of political prisoners. I grew up during the transition years, and, let me tell you, it ain't pretty) - but what I am saying is that communism was the counterbalance for capitalism. Governments had to keep their workers happy, lest the communist wave grew and, with it, the power of Moscow. 
The Scandinavian solution is socialist capitalism ( though we do see a weakening of the workers' rights here as well - the working poor phenomena has reached Norway, the richest Scandinavian country) and we see its prestige growing and growing. Is this the flat circle getting to the point where Scandinavia shapes the Western world again? Is this the solution the Western world is searching for? Not the elites, but the every day John and Jane. What do you guys think?

### **@l_i_f_e_force**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=Ugw6wNqwuuZeCDIRPth4AaABAg)

About the size of ships - you had different kind of ships - some where 40 meters long and had a crew of 100 people, which makes them really large at time. Other ships where wider and made for more storage - the word stocks comes from a scandinavian word for storage of goods - and other ships was smaller to traverse up and down smaller rivers - so the bigger ones would sail down rivers and when the river got shallower they would stop and load the goods onto smaller viking ships going further down the river. Also the ships didnt have keels so they could easily be rowed onto beaches and up shallow rivers. Sometimes they would also drag the boats over lands if the river dried out. The same ship would also be used to cross the atlantic ocean to Iceland and Greenland and Canada. They also sailed to north Africa - where they called the locals "skrælinger" or blåserker. They also called the native americans for skrælinger. Which they traded with.

### **@johnklaer2626**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgxRNO4Zcd7JjweaiHN4AaABAg)

One of the primary reasons that gave momentum to Viking exploration from the beginning is the nature of Nordic or Germanic culture.  Fathers didn't provide land or give up their land to sons upon reaching adulthood. Sons often had to look elsewhere for tillable land. Tillable land in the Nordic geography was limited in narrow stretches along the coast. That forced younger consecutive generations to look to the sea for better land, just beyond the horizon.  The Nordic pagan religion looked upon other peoples as targets, strangers to be overcome with less value rather than a sense of cooperation. Violence and death as warrior were glorified as an integral part of Nordic culture, not just as an odd, extraordinary circumstance required in war. The lack of modern equipment, techniques, at that time, to address living conditions in the bitter cold Nordic areas were primitive, requiring more callous hard-boiled behavior than the softer more effeminate features of good wine, gourmet food, modest climate and engineered running water, etc. within Roman empire.  The Romans can be severe but remain in what they saw as civilized conditions.  Trade naturally developed from these Viking sea travels, as word got back. Trade became more valuable and effective than just brutalizing others for its own sake. Keep in mind that this culture of severe violence aided their future conquests as adversaries decided to simply roll over and play dead so to speak. From the fury of the Northmen, deliver us oh Lord. Gradually the Vikings decided to settle down in other areas. Adversaries learned to create better defensive strategies. Vikings learned that becoming Christian had advantages. Christian morality eventually consumed the Vikings ending their violent predispositions, and the Viking age.

Long set of rebuttals, divided into two parts:



1. In the Rus-Byzantine treaties, the Rus’ leaders and their druzhinas swear only by Perun and Veles, even as early as Oleg, and just like the Primary Chronicle, the treaties only contain the Slavic forms of their names. The aforementioned treaties and chronicles differentiate the Varangians and the Rus’ as two separate, unrelated peoples. An immediate example appears in the retelling of the events of 905, when the twelve warbands of the Rus are assembled by Oleg, only one Warband is Scandinavian, and they are Variag/Varangian, and they are clearly stated as separate from the grouping, and notion of the Rus, who are all those twelve warbands, the majority being the Slavs and the last two, Finno-Ugrian, not to mention that during the signing of the Rus-Roman treaty of 907, the Rus/Rurikid and Varangian signatories are listed separately as two separate, different people. Even the Germanic Sagas deem the Varangians as Germans, whereas the Rus are treated as foreigners.



2. There are no archaeological findings of Norse colonization of Early Russia, and there are no archaeological and historical mentions of the worship of Germanic gods in the Rus’. There is evidence of limited Norse-Slavic cohabitation, in the form of the Varangian District in Novgorod (the smallest district, to boot) and in Staraya Ladoga, and items of trade, unfortunately for Normanists, and those like you, are proof of trade, and not colonization. Not to mention the near-total absence of Danelaw-like laws and legal systems in the Early Russian state.



3. The Normanists rationalize the total absence of the worship of Germanic gods in the Rus’ by postulating that the Russes/Slavs, the Varangians, and the Greeks didn’t distinguish Thor from Perun, but that is refuted by the content of the Black Grave – remains of two Varangian men, their equipment, and a religious, dark-bronze idol of Thor, which doesn’t just refute the aforementioned Normanist premise, but also proves that Thor was worshiped as a separate deity and not equated to Perun and that Thor’s worship was limited solely to the Norse Varangians.



4. All archaeological material that reveals Old Norse making was supplanted out of the material culture of the Early Russian elite by items of East Slavic making already by the mid-to-late tenth century and that they utterly disappeared in the eleventh century, which is further corroborated by the historical argument of the appearance of purely Slavic personal names among the immediate ruling Rurikid family – Sviatoslav, Predslava, and Volodislav, as attested in the Rus-Byzantine treaty of 944. After Igor, all historical Rurikids bore strictly Slavic names.



5. In the Primary Chronicle, the most integral paragraph regarding the origin of the Rurikids is found in the Laurentian Text, page 63, and it states the following: “Paul is the teacher of the Slavic race, from which we Russians too are sprung, even so, the Apostle Paul is the teacher of us Russians, for he preached to the Slavic nation, and appointed Andronicus as Bishop and successor to himself among them. But the Slavs and the Russes are one people, for it is because of the Varangians that the latter became known as Rus', though originally, they were Slavs. It summarily verifies that the Rus’ were originally Slavs, and further states that their involvement with the Varangians at one point in history is the reason why they were renamed as Rus’.



6. DNA studies have revealed and verified the Slavic origin of the Rurikids. All historical Rurikids (Gleb Sviatoslavich, Yaroslav Osmomysl, and Izyaslav Ingvarevich) who were tested were revealed to have predominantly Slavic aDNA profile, Slavic Y-DNA, and a total or near-total absence of Norse DNA. The oldest and the most indicative historical Rurikid is Gleb Sviatoslavich, Yaroslav’s paternal grandson, and bearer of the Y-DNA I2a1b-Din, an overwhelmingly Slavic aDNA profile with a particular, Mediterranean input which he had inherited from his mother or grandmother, which categorically confirms his status as Yaroslav’s grandson, whereas the modern paternal descendants of Yaroslav the Wise’s firstborn son, the house of Sviatopolk-Czetwertinski – the paternal progeny of Yaroslav’s firstborn son (with his second wife Saint Ingegerd) Iziaslav too are bearers of Y-DNA I2a1b-Din, that just further verifies the aforementioned – that the Rurikids were Slavs, thus Slavic Y-DNA. At this stage, all we need is Yaroslav’s own Y-DNA, and it is done. > 7. Originally, before the founding of the Normanist theory (and even after), the Rurikids were denoted as a Slavic dynasty, and the theories were the result of ethnographic and historical studies and not the weaponization of the Legend of the Invitation. The summaries of the theories are the following:
  
> 
  
> a) Varangians from Wagrians or Prussians
  
> The Austrian notable Sigismund Herberstein, being an adviser to the ambassador to Muscovy in the first half of the 16th century, was one of the first Europeans to get acquainted with the Russian chronicles and expressed his opinion about the origin of the Varangians and Rurik. Linking the name of the Varangians with the Slavo-Baltic people of the Wagri, Sigismund von Herberstein comes to the conclusion that: “Russians summoned their princes rather from the Wagri, or Varangians, than handed over power to foreigners who differ from them in faith, customs, and language". The Scandinavians and the Germans called the Wagri and all the Pomeranian Slavs the Wends. Synchronous sources do not contain information about the connection of the Pomeranian Slavs with the Varangians, although, in the 2nd half of the 10th century, Wendish naval campaigns against their neighbors were a noted sight.
  
> 
  
> b) Lomonosov denotes Rurik and the Varangians as having descended from the Prussian lands, relying on toponyms and later chronicles, which replaced the lexeme “Varangians” with the pseudo-ethnonym “Germans”. Lomonosov “a priori” accepted the Slavic origin of Rurik as an irrefutable fact: "The Varangians and Rurik with their clan, who came to Novgorod, were Slavic tribes, spoke the Slavic language, came from ancient Russians/Slavs, and were by no means from Scandinavia, but lived on the eastern-southern shores of the Varangian Sea, between the Vistula and Dvina rivers ... Scandinavia and on the northern shores of the Varangian Sea is nowhere to be heard ... Our chroniclers mention that Rurik and his Rod came from Nemets, and in some cases, it is written that from Prussia ... Between the Vistula and Dvina rivers, it flows into the Varangian Sea from the east-south side of the river, which above, near the city of Grodna, it is called Nemen, and Rusa is reputed to its mouth. Here it is clear that the Varangians-Rus lived in the eastern-southern coast of the Varangian Sea, near the Ruse River ... And the very name Prussians or Poruss shows that the Prussians lived along the Russes or alongside the Russes. Lomonosov. - "Objections to Miller's Dissertation"
  
> 
  
> c) Mecklenburg genealogies
  
> There is a folk legend about Rurik and his brothers, published in the 1830s by the French traveler and writer Xavier Marmier in the book "Northern Letters". He recorded that in Northern Germany, among the Mecklenburg peasants, formerly vigorous, by that time, almost completely Germanized. The legend tells that in the 8th century, the tribe of the Udrites was ruled by a king named Godlove, the father of three sons, the first of whom was called Rurik Mirny, the second - Sivar the Victorious, the third - Truvar the Faithful... The brothers decided to search for glory in the lands to the east. After many deeds and terrible battles, the brothers came to Russia, whose people suffered under the burden of a long tyranny but did not dare to rebel. The encouraging brothers awoke the lulling courage in the local people, led the army, and overthrew the rule of the oppressors. Having restored peace and order in the country, the brothers decided to return to their venerable father, but the grateful people begged them not to leave and take the place of the former kings. Thus, Rurik received the Novgorod principality (Nowoghorod), Sivar - Pskov (Pleskow), Truvar - Belozerskoe (Bile-Jezoro). After a while, the younger brothers died without leaving any trueborn heirs. Rurik annexed their principalities to his own, becoming the founder of the ruling dynasty. It should be noted that this is the only mention of Rurik in “Western” folklore, although the date of the legend cannot be established. The legend was written down a century after the publication of Rurik's Mecklenburg genealogy: "At the beginning of the 18th century, several genealogical works appeared on the dynasties of the Northern German lands of Mecklenburg, the former area of the settlement of Slavic tribes of “cheer” or “vigor”. In 1716, the vice-rector of the gymnasium, Friedrich Thomas, published work for the wedding of the Duke of Mecklenburg Karl Leopold and the Russian princess Catherine, daughter of Tsar Ivan V. Thomas used a 1687 manuscript written by the notary of the Mecklenburg court count Johann Friedrich von Chemnitz, who, in turn, referred to then the manuscript of 1418."
  
> 
  
> d) The 19th-century historian Gedeonov suggested that Rurik is not his given name, but the generic nickname Rerek, which was worn by all representatives of the ruling dynasty of the Obodrites. The assumption was substantiated by reference to the Scandinavian sagas, where allegedly the skald Guthorm Sindri calls the Wendish Slavs “falcons”. The quoted passage refers to the saga of Hakon the Good from the "Earth Circle" by Snorri Sturluson. Guthorm Sindri speaks of “falcon distance" In the context of the war in the 2nd half of the 10th century, King Hakon with the Vikings, both Danes and the Wends. In the Scandinavian sagas, the Wends (Pomeranian Slavs) began to engage in piracy from the 10th century; in earlier times, sources record only their land-based campaigns. Subsequently, the author of "The Lay of Igor's Host" calls the adults of Rurik falcons and the prince's falcons, but this epithet has been applied since ancient times to people of noble origin.
  
>

Your point about Odin being worshipped by the Wends is disingenuous; the only group of Wends that worshipped Odin were the Wendish Jomsvikings, whereas the traditional major deities of the Wends were Perun, Veles, and Svetovid. Furthermore, one of the leading authorities on the history of the Baltic Region, Matti Klinge, furthers the theory that the Rurikids were Wagrian Slavs and not Norsemen.

Something most historians are overlooking when it comes to Vikings (probably because most historians that study Vikings are Christians) are the fact about how much Christianity plays in to how the Viking age actually started. And it was much more a religious war then many like to say it is, actually many historians get aggressive when you mention this fact..
In many of the Viking sagas, it is clear(before they lost the religious war and become Christians them self) how much they disliked the Christians.
The main reason i think they lost the religious war, is that the Viking kings discovered how much easier it was to control people with the Christian unified religion then it was to control those with a religion with many Gods. So they saw it as a great tool to get power. And this is pretty clear when you see how many of them used it.

But.. As you already mention, the Vikings was there and had their culture long before the attack on Lindisfarne in 793.
What was different then, was that they came in warships, and not in their traditional trading ships they used before that.
And what triggered that? (many historians like to say overpopulation, but that is just silly)

What happen in Europe before this? It had become heavy Christian, and they had a war against pagan people where for example whole villages in to days Germany was slaughtered, simply because they was not Christians. This was of course not popular among the pagan population(and they had good contact with each other, so rumors about this spread fast) But that alone was not the reason, but probably one of many reasons that they already did not like the Christians.
But then, and that is probably the main reason, the Christians banned trade with pagans...
Just like the Chinese more or less refused to trade with the Mongols(and i think most who see this video know how that ended when the Mongols united against them..)
And that is why it was not just because of the gold and silver that they liked specially to attack monasteries, they knew very well that it was the Christians that had forbidden their traditional trade with many European countries.
And that was when they started to build war ships (know as Viking ships) in stead of the traditional trading ships they used before that.

And with that they started to discover more and more how easy it was to take in stead of trading in many cases.

When it comes to if the Vikings cared about if a man slept with a man, again, more then one saga mention how someone talk badly and put another person in a very bad light by saying he slept with men also. And this was seen as a great shame. For example in one of the tings in Iceland, in the Egils saga, one shief is made fun of by another when he say " many people know that you make a woman of your self when you once every month visit (a man i don`t remember the name of) and let him take you as if you was a woman.
Being seen as a real man was very important for Vikings, and if you let another man take you, you would lose respect at once. (but i understand that it is not political correct to be honest about this topic to day, so many pretend that it has always been accepted, even if this is not true at all. And maybe you even get fired as a teacher if you do not lie about this?)

Having said this, i think everything you say is also true. But to not mention this(the religious war part), is kind of like talking about world economy, and forget to mention oil.

Rather than defeating the Vikings then giving them Normandy, King Charles the Simple offered it to make them stop pillaging and plundering Paris, which was an attractive target. The offer came with 2 conditions: 1. stop other Vikings sailing down Seine River 2. convert to Christianity. They honoured both. 

More fun facts: the Vikings carried out the first and the second crusades, essentially. The first was conquering Sicily per the pope's request. The second was England because they elected their own bishop instead of the pope anointing one and the money stopped flowing into Rome. HOWEVER, despite all the wealth being hoarded in the city of London, it was untouched by the Normans, which brings up the possibility the order really originated from the central bank in its primitive form in the city of London and they also funded the Normans and their conquest. 

To this day, the city of London corporation has independence, sovereignty, own legal and tax systems, etc. from England or the UK or the EU. The current King of UK Charles III even cannot enter the Bank of England without permission.

They actually came from the same peninsula that is now shared by modern day Denmark and Germany and were one of three sister tribes the Angles, the Saxons and the Jutes that lived side-by-side on the northern German Peninsula shared with modern day, Denmark. The Angles and the Saxon’s first traveled south along with other Germanic tribes from Scandinavia and North Germany that went into what is modern day Germany, France, northern Italy, and Spain. Then around the first and second century, they crossed the English Channel from Germany into Great Britain and pushed the Celtic tribes north and west creating what is modern day, England (aka “Angland”). There were many dramatic tribes that were originally from Scandinavia in the German lands. These Scandinavian/dramatic tribes included the Franks, the Goths, the Visigoths, the Saxons, the Angles, the Marcomanni, the Lombardi, the Alemanni, the Suevi, Bavars, etc. but when the Vikings of the 700s, 800s through 1100 AD came down from Denmark, and Sweden, they met their sister tribes who had separated from them 1000 years before. Nevertheless, the language they spoke, then was still very similar.

The sagas, written centuries after the supposed events, are nothing more than stories, essentially the medieval version of Netflix scripts: entertaining, dramatic, but not hard evidence. Finding boats built a certain way in Scandinavia doesn’t prove the existence of a unified “Viking people”; shipbuilding styles can spread and be copied by raiders or pirates from many regions. In reality, these were likely scattered pirate groups from all over the world, not one single civilization. The term “Viking” itself is a manufactured label, while the iconic horned helmets are a complete 19th-century invention from costume designers, not archaeology. Even Hitler, who sent archaeologists across the globe searching for Aryan origins, never leaned on the sagas of the so-called Vikings — because he knew they were unreliable myths. What we think of today as “Vikings” is largely a modern obsession, fueled not by facts but by pop-culture brainwashing from shows and movies like Netflix’s dramatizations.

### **@nsjorgen** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgxxH3em6gsTXO8ZSqh4AaABAg)

You are missing one VERY key detail on the beginning of the Viking Age
In 783, the Franks under the rule of Charlemagne, massacred the pagan Saxons, and destroyed all their holy temples and trees (Irminsul). 
Many Saxons fled to Denmark, who soon after were building huge defenses along their southern border to guard against Frankish advance (Danevirke).
Denmark was a rally point for the Vikings, and a hub of their trade. News of this spread throughout the Viking World, that their way of life was under threat.
The Franks also embargoed the Vikings, and forbid trade with them, something they pressed upon their fellow Christians in Britain. 
This RUINED Viking Commerce. 

This is important knowledge to understand why Christian Monasteries where the first targets of the Vikings, as Lindisfarne held much more religious significance than it did economic.

4:40 mistake. The Magyars (Hungarians) were not Proto-Indo-European they were actually Finno-Ugric

> **@avatarion**  
> [View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgyPNCGJ8DVvbWatMSx4AaABAg.AUPg7Jh3Lm4AUicMP8-boH)  
>
> Finno-Ugric is a modern term. There was no name for anything like that. The Magyars back then were basically just a continuation of steppe cultures.  

> **@betwixtX**  
> [View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgyPNCGJ8DVvbWatMSx4AaABAg.AUPg7Jh3Lm4AUiwtcU4pfn)  
>
> @avatarion you are mixing up culture with linguistics. You are right that "Finno-Ugric" is a modern academic label, but you are wrong to imply that the Magyars were Indo-European just because they lived like other steppe people. Linguistic "DNA" doesn't change: While the name "Finno-Ugric" is modern, the language structure is ancient. Hungarian is related to Finnish and Mansi in ways that it will never be related to Indo-European languages like Latin or Slavic.  The Magyars were definitely part of the Steppe Culture—they rode horses, used composite bows, and lived in yurts just like the Huns, Avars, or Mongols. However, "Steppe Culture" is a lifestyle, not a single ethnic or linguistic group. Both Indo-Europeans (like the Scythians) and Uralic/Turkic peoples lived that way. By the time the Magyars entered Europe, they were a tribal confederation. They had absorbed plenty of Turkic and Iranian (Indo-European) influences, but their core identity and language remained distinctively Uralic.

### **@numenoreaneternity6682** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgzQpRdjD5ghFq9y36t4AaABAg)

There is no point in erroneously attempting to equate the Varangians to the Rus', for the Normanist theory is just that, a theory; the only reason why it is regarded as “fact-based” is because of its sociopolitical potency against the West’s longstanding enemy - Russia. The Varangians have only played a minor part in the founding of Russia, and they shouldn't be confused with the Rus', for even the Primary Chronicle (the Laurentian Codex) treats them as two separate, unrelated entities (the Primary Chronicle states that their homeland is between the Poles and the Baltic Prussians). Their impact in Ukraine, Belarus, and Russia is negligible at best, for the Varangians who originally arrived in Russia were diffuse, and furthermore, there are none of their towns, forts, or temples found in the aforementioned states – the sole exception to that rule being the Varangian district in Novgorod. If the Varangians had left a "great impact", then why is there next-to-no evidence of Norse colonization of Eastern Europe? Neither have they formed the nobility of the Rus', for the native, tribal nobility was already present and fully integrated into the Rus’ state, and were, and still are, predominantly the bearers of the haplogroups that are of the West-Eurasian Slavic origin. Not even the Rurikids were Varangian, for Rurik and his family were of the Rus'. The Primary Chronicle, the leading source on Early Russian and Rurikid history, states that the Varangians and the Rus' weren't the same, but separate people, that the Slavs and the Rus’ are one people, and that the Rus’ were originally Slavs (page 63). Not even the Germanic Sagas denote the Rus as Germanic. Even DNA studies state that the historical Rurikids, those who were direct paternal descendants of Rurik, weren't Norsemen, for they were bearers of the following haplogroups - of which the first two are the primary paternal lineages among Slavs: the haplogroup I2a1b - the clan of the princes Svyatopolk-Chetvertinskikh (who would later assume the position of one of the thirteen magnates houses of the PLC), ascending to the Turovopin Rurikovich-Izyaslavichi, and the lineage of Gleb Sviatoslavich – the historical I2a1b-Din specimen, and the oldest confirmed and tested historical Rurikid (paternal grandson of Yaroslav the Wise), his father Sviatoslav II and his brother Iziaslav (modern, paternal relatives tested for I2a1b-Din), through their father Yaroslav, who was the son of Vladimir, who was the son of Sviatoslav, who was the son of Igor – the sole surviving son of Rurik, and the bearers of the haplogroup R1a-M458 – the clan of the Verkhov princes with the historical specimen Izyaslav Ingvarevich. There are even entire Rurikid houses that bear N1c1, but all N1c1 findings are modern, are not present among historical Rurikids – suggesting a non-paternity event, and are derived from Vsevolod, who is therefore presumed to have been fathered not by Yaroslav, but by Saint Olaf, which correlates with the period of Vsevolod’s conception.



Furthermore, Oleg of Novgorod was the prince of the Rus', not the Varangians. The adjective "Varangian" was a purported demonym added to the men of the Rurikid dynasty by 18th-19th-century Russian, Swedish, and German Normanists. The Primary Chronicle relates that in the year 6415 (907 AD), the Rus prince Oleg made a peace treaty with the Byzantine Empire by taking his men to the shrines and swearing by their weapons and by their god Perun, and by Veles, they confirmed the treaty. We find the same form of confirmation of a peace treaty by Prince Igor in 945 and by Prince Sviatoslav in 971. In 980, when Prince Vladimir the Great came to the throne of Kyiv, he erected statues of five pagan gods in front of his palace, which he soon thereafter discarded after his Christianization in 988. Perun was chief among these, represented with a silver head and a golden mustache. Vladimir's uncle Dobrinja also had a shrine of Perun established in his city of Novgorod. After the Christianization of the Rus’, this site became a monastery, which, quite remarkably, continued to bear the name of Perun. There is not a single temple found in Russia dedicated to any Norse god, let alone toponyms. The Normanists rationalize that by claiming that the Russes/Slavs, the Varangians, and the Greeks didn’t distinguish Thor from Perun, but that is refuted by the findings of the Black Grave – remains of two Varangian men, their equipment, and a religious, dark-bronze idol of Thor, which doesn’t just refute the aforementioned thesis, but also proves that Thor was worshiped as a separate deity, and only by the Norse Varangians. Not just that, but propagating the idea that the Rus' was the result of the involvement of foreign, Norse-like people is both erroneous and opposed to the fundamental rules of the founding of organized nations. In 1914, the Swedish archaeologist T. J. Arne argued for a mass Viking-age Scandinavian colonization of Eastern Europe. Arne's theories remained largely unchallenged until the 1940s, when anti-Normanism, in part a reaction to the Nazi invasion of the Soviet Union, was proclaimed official Soviet state dogma. The Postwar USSR witnessed a golden age for Soviet archaeology, with the state sponsorship of thousands of archaeological excavations. Key to the anti-Normanist position were the excavations at Gnezdovo and Staraya Ladoga, near Smolensk and Novgorod, respectively. Normanists considered both to be Scandinavian settlements, but Soviet archaeologists (Artsikhovsky, Avdusin, Ravdonikas) have proven that there is minimal evidence of Northern Germanic colonization at these sites. Based on the aforementioned and further empirical proof gathered by veritable historians and archaeologists, it is morally and intellectually necessary to abandon dogmatic “conventions”, for they’ve proven to be categorically, or at least largely false, thus why the "aforementioned conventions" made by those who ignore opposing proof for the sake of the furthering of their people’ agenda and interests, like the Swedes and Germans, are of arbitrary worth to history. History is an ever-expanding and ever-growing science – one that actively revises the parts that were proven to be inaccurate or outdated. Empirical proof trumps "conventions", it is a shame that the Normanists have traditionally refused to understand that. 



A detail of utmost value that everyone either omits or dismisses is that the earliest Muslim sources, such as Ibn Khurradadhbih, explicitly denote the Rus to be 'one of the Slavic peoples. The whole mantra that Rurikids are Norsemen (as opposed to the preceding, original theories that denoted them as Slavs) starts with the Normanist theories in the 18th century, furthered by the Holstein-Gottorp Romanovs and Gerhard Friedrich Muller, and was opposed by most sane-minded members of the Russian intelligentsia, even by paternal Rurikids themselves (like Vasily Nikitich Tatishchev). This is the reason why Normanists rely on abstract authority like "consensus" and "accordance", and adhere to "conventions” and “established authorities” rather than conclusive proof, for they're aware of the fact that their theory is not substantiated by the latter. It’s also paramount to note that proof should not be confused with evidence, for the former is conclusive, while the latter isn’t. Normanist historiography traditionally prefers the ambiguity of liberal interpretations supported by argumentum ad Populum, and by consensus by antonomasia to the conclusiveness of proof, due to its openness to favorable interpretation. That isn’t just a sign of an exhausted school of thought, but of one that cannot defend its assessments, due to how profoundly they’re being refuted by archaeological excavations, recent results of Rurikid paternal lineages uncovered by molecular biology, and even contemporary proof from that era, like the aforementioned treaties. While we're on the issue of the treaties, Oleg was Rurik's immediate successor (since he was immediate, the argument of “Slavicization” is moot), so why was he, as an alleged Norseman, swearing to Slavic gods like Perun and Veles instead of the Norse Gods? Matter of fact, why were all Rurikids swearing by Perun and Veles, if they were, according to Normanists, not Slavs?

### **@ichuakin1**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgyaFdbJD5WGs_YT7tt4AaABAg)

If you include Vikings, you can't let out Celtics and Phoenicians. As they set the foundations of coastal exploration simultaneously, Celtics spread metallurgy across continental Europe, while Phoenicians spread the alphabet all around the Mediterranean. I think those two events are a major milestone in the development of human capabilities through history, and often they don't get the appreciation they deserve.
In one hand, metal work represented a great advance in everyday duties, as agriculture or handcraft, but most importantly, in the military. As Gladius wouldn't exist without Celtic influence in Iberia, either Roman empire wouldn't exist without Celtic culture, neither the Nordic. 
On the other hand, it's absurd to talk about the critical role of alphabet  in the development of both Greek and Roman mythologies. My point is the significance of the alphabet in terms of social structure and territorial administration. Before it's appearance, the most widespread form of population was the city-state, in the form of greek Polis and the celtic "-briga", with a clan hierarchy. However, with the arrival of alphabet, this shifted into major States, as a written standard language is more efficient for administration than any form of spoken dialectical variation of it. This later set the basis for national identity and Imperialism, as it allowed Greek to develop as a "Lingua Franca" and establish itself in opposition to foreigners (barbaroi). This is the first time in European history this idea appears, but it is not gonna be the last one for sure...
So it's 7 pillars in my humble oppinion.

### **@gunnarthegumbootguy7909**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7k5XoLzwmls&lc=UgzTrDYDT-j5h0PguK14AaABAg)

hmm while the broad-picture view presented here is interesting there are a bunch of factual errors and somethings that are explained in a way that is easy to misunderstand. Like the sagas they were written down in iceland yes but for the most part they weren't from iceland, many of them were stories that had circulated in scandinavia and in some cases all of the germanic areas for centuries.
As for the stuff about printing books and monestaries, this all happened much later, he mentions printing and fonts, but these raids on monestaries happened about 700 years before printing was invented in Europe. Printing had been invented in China but that technology hadn't been transferred to europe and european printing was later invented independently hundreds of years after the end of the viking period, and specifically undermined monestaries as a main source of books). 

And they would not have tolerated men having sex with eachother, actually one of the worst accusations for the old norse was being "argr" (basically an effeminate man or a "not real man"), and this accusation had to be defended either in a court, or forcing the accuser to take back his words (though that was rare because then the accuser took on the status of argr himself for falsely calling someone else an argr, or you could simply fight the one who accused you of being argr to death or to the point he had to concede and take back the accusation, this proved your manliness and was the socially preferred option. A man who accepted to have another man have sex with him would have been the argr, not the man who was "on top", except maybe if he did it with specific pleasure and prefered doing this more than with women, but mainly it was the man who took the "position of a woman" who could be accused, and anyone even allowing themself to be called argr and not challenging it at all would be declared as not being a real man, which meant he couldn't marry or take any vows/contracts, and wouldn't be allowed to do any male-role things and would probably not survive long.

As for women's high status it is true, but wasn't mainly because that they were allowed to do magic, they had one set of magic that was female only and this was certainly the more commonly used type of magic, but men were also allowed to do magic, but these were about different things and involved completely different methods and had different words. The main reason women had high status is they women were the owners of the house, not just in some figural sense, but they were the one with the key, they decided who could be invited to enter the house and how much anyone got to eat including the husband, they were in charge of milking, and taking care of tame animals cooking and butchering, brewing mead or ale, while men where involved in grain farming, fishing and hunting, so because of their ties to the household was strong and being the owner of the household they were in high status and they could also take on generally high status in their local community as well.


Generally I think if you make such a presentation and make wide claims about how vikings was a major european culture (which i think is absolutely the case), before doing this, it would be better to get specific facts right first, or be more careful when you are not certain.

Research into the viking age has been systematically shut down for generations. First by christian academics who saw it as at best childish fantasy, at worst a front for normalizing heathenry. Then by the secularists who saw it as at best childish fantasy, at worst a front for normalizing white supremacy because of a small insignificant white supremacist prisom gang in the us "odinists" who uses their symbolism but doesnt practice anything affiliated with nordic tradition. They also draw arbitrary lines between hitlers fascism and nordic philosophy, hitler and his advisors were world occult enthusiasts, they studied all kinds of metaphysical schools, pretty much all of them they had available. They had no special emphasis on nordic study or practice. If anything they looked more to arabic, christian, jewish occult, as well as all manner of european, asian, african and indigenous american folk traditions. So the distrust and hatred of viking studies is pretty baseless. If anything, by stigmatizing it, guarantees that only bad faith actors, extremists, supremacists get to own it and only other counterposed studies into the era are shut down.

For me i am a neopagan, agnostic folkist, of scottish-scandinavian and sicilian ancestry. I study and practice an eclectic unitarian faith, and use nordic symbolism, critical retellings(as post contact corruptions) of the sagas, and modern renditions of traditions passed down. I am also at home in various orthodox and unorthodox christian churches, because i see parrallels between the stories and suspect much more "pre contact" contact, or back and forth migrations during the bronze and later stone ages where the proto-indo-european ideas that became the various world teachings, blending with local indegenous teachings and environmental pressures.

The vikings played a huge role in this in europe during the heart if the medieval era shrouded in manufactured darkness. There is a lot of powerful interests, religious and secular, who want us to remain ignorant about this era. The viking lineage has always represented an intuitive folk symbol of resistance and liberation, as much as power tries to slander them and turn them into a joke or terrible people.

Whether or not nordic reconstructionism is a pathway to understanding european history and liberation, the study of that era from 700-1200ad is essential to understanding european medievalism, the general trends of what defined the so called dark ages, how early european, proto modern power structures emerged, and especially how the stories told then and now are used to divide and control, justify the aweful deeds of competing power holders.
